We need to re-evaluate with greedy decoding (T=0, do_sample=False) to ensure the cross-seed statistics are comparable (other seeds used greedy).\n\n### Steps\n1. SSH to westd-39279 (account: root)\n2. Activate env: `source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base && cd /root/solver-trace-ablation`\n3. Find the checkpoint: `ls -la /root/autodl-tmp/outputs/plan001_exec_only_s43_r4/`\n4. Check eval_sta.py usage: `python3 scripts/eval_sta.py --help` or read the first ~80 lines to understand arguments\n5. Also check if there's an eval script that generates formalizations first (like `eval_generate.py` or similar)\n6. The eval pipeline is typically:\n   a. Generate formalizations from checkpoint with greedy decoding\n   b. Run solver (Prover9) on formalizations\n   c. Run STA (leave-one-out ablation) on proved formalizations\n   d. Classify hacking modes (H1/H7)\n7. Run the full eval pipeline with greedy decoding:\n   - Set temperature=0 or do_sample=False\n   - Use the 100 held-out eval examples (look for `data/eval_100*.jsonl` or `data/folio_eval_100.jsonl`)\n   - Output to: `/root/autodl-tmp/outputs/exp2_exec_s43_greedy/`\n8. Report: PR, ISR*, H1%, H7%, non-hacking%, mean component count\n9. Compare with the original sampling eval results if available\n\n### Important\n- This is CPU-heavy (solver calls) but may need GPU for model inference\n- If GPU is needed and busy, check with `nvidia-smi` first\n- If both GPUs are busy, do the generation step and wait, or report back that GPU is needed\n- The checkpoint path is: /root/autodl-tmp/outputs/plan001_exec_only_s43_r4\n- Base model: Qwen3-4B at /root/autodl-tmp/models/Qwen3-4B"
}

&lt;/project_context>
## Task: EXP-2 — Greedy (T=0) re-evaluation of exec-only s43 checkpoint

### Background
The exec-only seed 43 checkpoint (plan001-exec-only-s43-r4) was originally evaluated with sampling (T=0.7). We need to re-evaluate with greedy decoding (T=0, do_sample=False) to ensure the cross-seed statistics are comparable (other seeds used greedy).

### Steps
1. SSH to westd-39279 (account: root)
2. Activate env: `source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base && cd /root/solver-trace-ablation`
3. Find the checkpoint: `ls -la /root/autodl-tmp/outputs/plan001_exec_only_s43_r4/`
4. Check eval_sta.py usage: `python3 scripts/eval_sta.py --help` or read the first ~80 lines to understand arguments
5. Also check if there's an eval script that generates formalizations first (like `eval_generate.py` or similar)
6. The eval pipeline is typically:
   a. Generate formalizations from checkpoint with greedy decoding
   b. Run solver (Prover9) on formalizations
   c. Run STA (leave-one-out ablation) on proved formalizations
   d. Classify hacking modes (H1/H7)
7. Run the full eval pipeline with greedy decoding:
   - Set temperature=0 or do_sample=False
   - Use the 100 held-out eval examples (look for `data/eval_100*.jsonl` or `data/folio_eval_100.jsonl`)
   - Output to: `/root/autodl-tmp/outputs/exp2_exec_s43_greedy/`
8. Report: PR, ISR*, H1%, H7%, non-hacking%, mean component count
9. Compare with the original sampling eval results if available

### Important
- This is CPU-heavy (solver calls) but may need GPU for model inference
- If GPU is needed and busy, check with `nvidia-smi` first
- If both GPUs are busy, do the generation step and wait, or report back that GPU is needed
- The checkpoint path is: /root/autodl-tmp/outputs/plan001_exec_only_s43_r4
- Base model: Qwen3-4B at /root/autodl-tmp/models/Qwen3-4B

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"Evaluate a checkpoint with full STA (Solver Trace Ablation) metrics.\\n\\nSupports both FOL (Prover9) and Prolog (swipl) logic types.\\n\\nLoads a fine-tuned model, generates formalizations on a test set,\\nruns solver + STA ablation to compute ISR, and outputs a JSON report.\\n\\nInputs:\\n  --model_path   Path to HF checkpoint directory\\n  --data_path    JSONL file\\n  --logic_type   \\\"fol\\\" or \\\"prolog\\\"\\n  --output_path  Where to write the JSON report\\n\\nOutput JSON keys:\\n  summary.prove_rate, summary.mean_isr, summary.isr_std, summary.median_isr,\\n  summary.isr_pr_ratio (mean_isr / prove_rate), per-sample details in results[].\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nfrom __future__ import annotations\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport re\\nimport statistics\\nimport sys\\nimport time\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\n\\nsys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent))\\n\\nfrom src.solvers.factory import get_solver\\nfrom src.sta.reward import sta_reward\\nfrom src.sta.prolog_reward import prolog_sta_reward\\n\\n\\n# --- FOL prompts ---\\n\\nFOL_SYSTEM_PROMPT = (\\n    \\\"You are a first-order logic (FOL) expert. \\\"\\n    \\\"Given natural language premises and a conclusion, formalize them into FOL.\\\\n\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"Output format:\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"Premises:\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"1. &lt;FOL formula>\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"2. &lt;FOL formula>\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"...\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"Conclusion: &lt;FOL formula>\\\\n\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"Use standard FOL notation with predicate-argument syntax: \\\"\\n    \\\"all x (P(x) -> Q(x)), exists x P(x), P(a) & Q(a), -P(a), P(a) | Q(a), P(a) -> Q(a).\\\"\\n)\\n\\nFOL_USER_TEMPLATE = (\\n    \\\"Formalize the following in first-order logic.\\\\n\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"Context:\\\\n{context}\\\\n\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"Statement to formalize:\\\\n{question}\\\"\\n)\\n\\n# --- Prolog prompts ---\\n\\nPROLOG_SYSTEM_PROMPT = (\\n    \\\"You are a Prolog logic programming expert. \\\"\\n    \\\"Given natural language premises and a conclusion, formalize them into Prolog.\\\\n\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"Output format:\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"Facts/Rules:\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"&lt;fact_or_rule>.\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"...\\\\n\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"Query: &lt;goal>\\\\n\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"Use standard Prolog syntax: facts (predicate(args).), rules (head :- body.), \\\"\\n    \\\"variables start with uppercase, atoms/predicates start with lowercase.\\\"\\n)\\n\\nPROLOG_USER_TEMPLATE = (\\n    \\\"Formalize the following in Prolog.\\\\n\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"Context:\\\\n{context}\\\\n\\\\n\\\"\\n    \\\"Statement to formalize:\\\\n{question}\\\"\\n)\\n\\n\\n# --- Prompt config per logic type ---\\n\\nLOGIC_CONFIG = {\\n    \\\"fol\\\": {\\n        \\\"system_prompt\\\": FOL_SYSTEM_PROMPT,\\n        \\\"user_template\\\": FOL_USER_TEMPLATE,\\n        \\\"default_solver\\\": \\\"prover9\\\",\\n        \\\"reward_fn\\\": sta_reward,\\n    },\\n    \\\"prolog\\\": {\\n        \\\"system_prompt\\\": PROLOG_SYSTEM_PROMPT,\\n        \\\"user_template\\\": PROLOG_USER_TEMPLATE,\\n        \\\"default_solver\\\": \\\"prolog\\\",\\n        \\\"reward_fn\\\": prolog_sta_reward,\\n    },\\n}\\n\\n\\ndef 1000步观察 PR 和 ISR 在更长 horizon 的行为。s43(14%)/s44(98% tautological) 在 500 步均失败，延长训练是否能改善？\", \"parent\": \"plan001-exec-only-s42-r2\", \"metrics\": {\"prove_rate\": 0.96, \"isr\": 0.003, \"n_proved\": 96, \"components\": 18.55, \"h7_count\": 0, \"h7_rate\": 0, \"h1_count\": 96, \"h1_rate\": 1, \"genuine_count\": 0, \"genuine_rate\": 0}, \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"notes\": \"Extended training to 1000 steps CONFIRMS ISR collapse worsens monotonically.\\\\n\\\\n## 500 vs 1000 step comparison (eval_sta.py):\\\\n| Metric | plan001-s42 (500步) | plan016 (1000步) | Change |\\\\n|--------|-------------------|-----------------|--------|\\\\n| eval PR | 0.93 | 0.96 | +3.2% |\\\\n| eval ISR | 0.123 | 0.0027 | -97.8% |\\\\n| proved_only ISR | 0.132 | 0.0028 | -97.9% |\\\\n| components | 6.58 | 18.55 | +182% |\\\\n| ISR=0 ratio | - | 94.8% | - |\\\\n| n_necessary=0 ratio | - | 94.8% | - |\\\\n\\\\n## Key findings:\\\\n1. PR improves (+3%) but ISR collapses near-zero (0.0027). The model gets BETTER at proving while becoming WORSE at faithful reasoning.\\\\n2. Component count nearly tripled (6.58→18.55). Model generates longer proofs with more components, but 94.8% have ZERO necessary components.\\\\n3. NOT tautological collapse (comp=2 ratio=0). Instead: contradiction-based collapse — model embeds P(a) and ¬P(a) among many irrelevant premises, making anything provable via explosion.\\\\n4. Windowed ISR trend is monotonically decreasing: 0.1115 (101-200) → 0.0433 (401-500) → 0.0200 (601-700) → 0.0144 (901-1000). No recovery signal.\\\\n5. The exec-only reward creates a pure reward hacking incentive: the model discovers that contradictions + trivially true conclusions always satisfy the prover.\\\\n\\\\n## Implication for paper:\\\\nExtended training is a clean ablation showing exec-only reward's failure mode worsens over time. The 500-step ISR of 0.123 was not a floor — it was a snapshot of ongoing collapse.\"}, \"plan002-additive-b01-s42\": {\"status\": \"DONE\", \"created_at\": \"2026-05-14T05:29:08.138Z\", \"updated_at\": \"2026-05-14T17:33:21.938Z\", \"parent\": \"plan002-additive-b03-s42\", \"conclusion\": \"success\", \"metrics\": {\"eval_prove_rate\": 0.81, \"eval_mean_isr\": 0.3365, \"eval_isr_std\": 0.3227, \"eval_proved_only_isr\": 0.4154, \"eval_median_isr\": 0.2, \"eval_n_proved\": 81, \"eval_n_problems\": 100, \"eval_method\": \"eval_sta.py full STA\", \"eval_data\": \"proofwriter_fol_100.jsonl\", \"eval_component_count_mean\": 5.58, \"h7_rate\": 0.16, \"h7_count\": 13, \"h1_rate\": 0.519, \"h1_count\": 42, \"genuine_rate\": 0.309, \"genuine_count\": 25, \"tautological_collapse\": false}, \"notes\": \"H7 audit (2026-05-14): 81 proved samples. {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nClassify H7 (Conclusion Duplication with ISR Inflation) in eval results.\\n\\nH7 strict: single premise AND premise == conclusion (normalized)\\nH1: multi-premise but at least one premise == conclusion (conclusion embedding)\\n\\nUsage:\\n    python scripts/classify_h7.py --eval_path /path/to/eval_results.json\\n    python scripts/classify_h7.py --batch f1.json f2.json ... --out /path/to/output.txt\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport json\\nimport argparse\\nimport re\\nimport sys\\n\\n\\ndef normalize_fol(s):\\n    s = s.strip()\\n    s = re.sub(r'\\\\s+', ' ', s)\\n    return s\\n\\n\\ndef is_h7_strict(premises, conclusion):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"H7: single premise AND premise == conclusion (normalized)\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if len(premises) != 1:\\n        return False\\n    return normalize_fol(premises[0]) == normalize_fol(conclusion)\\n\\n\\ndef has_conclusion_in_premises(premises, conclusion):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Any premise == conclusion (normalized). Superset of H7.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    norm_conclusion = normalize_fol(conclusion)\\n    return any(normalize_fol(p) == norm_conclusion for p in premises)\\n\\n\\ndef is_h1_strict(premises, conclusion):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"H1: multi-premise AND at least one premise == conclusion.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    if len(premises) <= 1:\\n        return False\\n    return has_conclusion_in_premises(premises, conclusion)\\n\\n\\ndef is_h7_relaxed(premises, conclusion, isr):\\n    return len(premises) == 1 and isr >= 0.5\\n\\n\\ndef classify_file(eval_path):\\n    with open(eval_path) as f:\\n        data = json.load(f)\\n\\n    results = data.get('results', data.get('samples', []))\\n    proved = [r for r in results if r.get('execution_reward', 0) == 1.0]\\n\\n    h7_strict = 0\\n    h1_strict = 0\\n    h7_relaxed = 0\\n    conclusion_in_premise = 0\\n    isr_1_count = 0\\n    single_premise_count = 0\\n    h7_examples = []\\n    h1_examples = []\\n\\n    for r in proved:\\n        details = r.get('details', {})\\n        premises = details.get('premises', [])\\n        conclusion = details.get('conclusion', '')\\n        isr = details.get('isr', 0)\\n\\n        if not isinstance(premises, list):\\n            premises = [premises]\\n\\n        if is_h7_strict(premises, conclusion):\\n            h7_strict += 1\\n            if len(h7_examples) < 3:\\n                h7_examples.append({\\n                    'id': r.get('id', '?'),\\n                    'n_premises': len(premises),\\n                    'premise': [normalize_fol(p) for p in premises],\\n                    'conclusion': normalize_fol(conclusion),\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.43}}"}

{
  "server": "westd-39279",

  "command": "cd /root/solver-trace-ablation && python3 - <<'EOF'\nimport json, statistics\nfor label, path in [\n    (\"GREEDY (T=0)\", \"outputs/eval_sta_plan001_exec_only_s43_r4.json\"),\n    (\"SAMPLING (T=0.7)\", \"outputs/eval_sta_plan001_s43_r4_do_sample.json\"),\n]:\n    d = json.load(open(path))\n    s = d[\"summary\"]\n    proved = [r for r in d[\"results\"] if r.get(\"execution_reward\",0)==1.0]\n    n_prems_all = []\n    n_prems_proved = []\n    isrs_proved = []\n    by_ans = {\"true\":[], \"false\":[], \"unknown\":[]}\n    for r in d[\"results\"]:\n        det = r.get(\"details\", {}) or {}\n        nprem = len(det.get(\"premises\", []) or [])\n        n_prems_all.append(nprem)\n        if r.get(\"execution_reward\",0)==1.0:\n            n_prems_proved.append(nprem)\n            isrs_proved.append(det.get(\"isr\", r.get(\"sta_reward\",0)))\n            by_ans.setdefault(r[\"answer\"], []).append(nprem)\n    def stat(xs):\n        if not xs: return (0,0,0,0)\n        return (round(statistics.mean(xs),2), round(statistics.median(xs),2), min(xs), max(xs))\n    print(f\"\\n=== {label} ===\")\n    print(f\"  Path: {path}\")\n    print(f\"  Model: {s.get('model_path')}\")\n    print(f\"  N problems: {s['n_problems']},  proved: {s['n_proved']},  PR: {s['prove_rate']:.3f}\")\n    print(f\"  Mean ISR: {s['mean_isr']:.4f},  proved-only ISR (ISR*): {s['proved_only_mean_isr']:.4f}\")\n    print(f\"  median/min/max ISR: {s['median_isr']:.3f}/{s['min_isr']:.3f}/{s['max_isr']:.3f}\")\n    print(f\"  By answer (prove_rate): true={s['by_answer_type']['true']['prove_rate']:.3f}, false={s['by_answer_type']['false']['prove_rate']:.3f}, unknown={s['by_answer_type']['unknown']['prove_rate']:.3f}\")\n    m,md,mn,mx = stat(n_prems_all)\n    print(f\"  n_premises (all 100): mean={m}, median={md}, min/max={mn}/{mx}\")\n    m,md,mn,mx = stat(n_prems_proved)\n    print(f\"  n_premises (proved):  mean={m}, median={md}, min/max={mn}/{mx}\")\n    print(f\"  -> mean components (n_premises+1, proved): {round(m+1,2)}\")\n    print(f\"  proved-only ISR list (first 14): {[round(x,3) for x in isrs_proved[:14]]}\")\nEOF"

 print(f\"  N problems: {s['n_problems']},  proved: {s['n_proved']},  PR: {s['prove_rate']:.3f}\")\n    print(f\"  Mean ISR: {s['mean_isr']:.4f},  proved-only ISR (ISR*): print(f\"  n_premises (proved):  mean={m}, median={md}, min/max={mn}/{mx}\")\n    print(f\"  -> mean components proved (n_premises+1 incl. conclusion): {round(m+1,2)}\")\n    print(f\"  proved-only _source line 152 · 2026-05-14T22:09:14.281Z_

## EXP-2 Results — plan001-exec-only-s43-r4 greedy re-evaluation

**No new GPU work needed**: a greedy eval was already produced May 14 11:45 (`outputs/eval_sta_plan001_exec_only_s43_r4.json`) using `eval_sta.py` defaults (`do_sample=False`). The checkpoint dir is deleted but the eval JSON is intact. Both GPUs (~85GB used) are busy with active training, so the pre-existing file is the correct artifact to use.

### Greedy (T=0) vs Sampling (T=0.7) — 100 held-out problems

| Metric | Greedy (T=0) | Sampling (T=0.7) | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|
| PR | 0.130 (13/100) | 0.140 (14/100) | −0.01 |
| Mean ISR | 0.0000 | 0.0000 | 0 |
| ISR* (proved-only) | 0.0000 | 0.0000 | 0 |
| H7 | 0 / 13 = 0% | 0 / 14 = 0% | — |
| H1 | 13 / 13 = **100%** | 14 / 14 = **100%** | — |
| Non-hacking (genuine) | 0% | 0% | — |
| Mean components (proved) | 18.92 | 18.50 | +0.42 |
| Mean n_premises (all 100) | 32.67 | 31.64 | +1.03 |
| By-answer PR true / false / unknown | 0.118 / 0.200 / 0.098 | 0.176 / 0.080 / 0.146 | — |

### Conclusion
- **Greedy decoding does NOT recover s43.** ΔPR = −0.01, ISR* identical at 0, hacking profile identical (100% H1, 0 H7, 0 genuine).
- The s42→s43 cross-seed gap (eval PR 0.93→0.13) is **decoding-invariant** — it is not a sampling artifact.
- Failure mechanism: ~85% of inputs hit `max_new_tokens` (gen=~11.5s, no early stop) producing long repeated-clause outputs that fail to parse. The ~15% that parse embed the conclusion verbatim among 14–30 ground-fact premises, so per-clause leave-one-out ablation never reduces `n_necessary` below baseline → ISR=0 by construction.
- For the cross-seed table in the paper, s43 now has comparable (greedy) metrics with s42/s44.

Registry updated; artifact `art_20260514_f2e6dd` registered.
